[P2105] 6.6.4.5.6.5	Protection of GPS
[P2106] In regions where FCC regulation applies, requirements for protection of GPS according to FCC Order DA 10-48 applies for operation in Band n24. The following normative requirement covers BS type 1-C or BS type 1-H operating in Band n24, to be used together with other information about the site installation to verify compliance with the requirement in FCC Order DA 10-48.
[P2107] The level of emissions in the 1541 – 1650 MHz band, measured in measurement bandwidth according to Table 6.6.4.5.6.5-1 shall not exceed the basic limits PEM_B24,a, PEM_B24,b, PEM_B24,c, PEM_B24,d, PEM_B24,e, and PEM_B24,f declared by the manufacturer.
[P2108] Table 6.6.4.5.6.5-1: Declared emissions basic limits for protection of GPS
[P2110] Note:	The regional requirements in FCC Order DA 20-48 are defined in terms of EIRP (effective isotropic radiated power), which is dependent on both the BS emissions at the antenna connector and the deployment (including antenna gain and feeder loss). The EIRP level is calculated using: PEIRP = PEM + Gant where PEM denotes the unwanted emission level at the antenna connector and Gant equals the RDN and antenna array gain.  The requirement defined above provides the characteristics of the base station needed to verify compliance with the regional requirement.
[P2111] 6.6.4.5.6.6	Additional operating band unwanted emissions limits for operation with shared spectrum channel access
[P2112] In addition, for operation with shared spectrum channel access, the BS may have to comply with the applicable operating band unwanted emission limits established regionally, when deployed in regions where those limits apply and under the conditions declared by the manufacturer. The regional requirements may be in the form of conducted power, power spectral density, EIRP and other types of limits. In case of regulatory limits based on EIRP, assessment of the EIRP level is described in Annex F.2.
[P2113] 6.6.4.5.6.7	Void
[P2114] 6.6.4.5.7	BS type 1-C
[P2115] The operating band unwanted emissions for BS type 1-C for each antenna connector shall be below the applicable basic limits defined in clauses 6.6.4.5.2 – 6.6.4.5.6.
[P2116] For Band n41 and n90 operation in Japan, the operating band unwanted emissions limits shall be applied to the sum of the emission power over all antenna connectors for BS type 1-C.
[P2117] 6.6.4.5.8	BS type 1-H
[P2118] The operating band unwanted emissions requirements for BS type 1-H are that for each TAB connector TX min cell group and each applicable basic limit in clauses 6.6.4.5.2 – 6.6.4.5.6, the power summation emissions at the TAB connectors of the TAB connector TX min cell group shall not exceed a BS limit specified as the basic limit + X, where X = 10log10(NTXU,countedpercell).
[P2119] NOTE:	Conformance to the BS type 1-H spurious emission requirement can be demonstrated by meeting at least one of the following criteria as determined by the manufacturer:
[P2120] 1)	The sum of the emissions power measured on each TAB connector in the TAB connector TX min cell group shall be less than or equal to the limit as defined in this clause for the respective frequency span.
[P2121] Or
[P2122] 2)	The unwanted emissions power at each TAB connector shall be less than or equal to the BS type 1-H limit as defined in this clause for the respective frequency span, scaled by -10log10(n), where n is the number of TAB connectors in the TAB connector TX min cell group.
[P2123] 6.6.5	Transmitter spurious emissions
[P2124] 6.6.5.1	Definition and applicability
[P2125] The transmitter spurious emission limits shall apply from 9 kHz to 12.75 GHz, excluding the frequency range from ΔfOBUE below the lowest frequency of each supported downlink operating band, up to ΔfOBUE above the highest frequency of each supported downlink operating band, where the ΔfOBUE is defined in table 6.6.1. For some operating bands, the upper limit is higher than 12.75 GHz in order to comply with the 5th harmonic limit of the downlink operating band, as specified in ITU-R recommendation SM.329 [5].
[P2126] For a multi-band connector, each supported operating band together with ΔfOBUE around the band is excluded from the transmitter spurious emissions requirement.
[P2127] The requirements shall apply whatever the type of transmitter considered (single carrier or multi-carrier). It applies for all transmission modes foreseen by the manufacturer's specification.
[P2128] The requirements shall apply to BS that support NR or NR with NB-IoT operation in NR in-band.
[P2129] Unless otherwise stated, all requirements are measured as mean power (RMS).
[P2130] For operation in region 2, where the FCC guidance for MIMO systems in FCC Title 47 [13] is applicable, NTXU,countedpercell shall be equal to one for the purposes of calculating the spurious emissions limits in clauses 6.6.5. For all other unwanted emissions requirements, NTXU,countedpercell shall be the value calculated according to clause 6.1.
[P2131] 6.6.5.2	Minimum requirement
[P2132] The minimum requirement applies per single-band connector, or per multi-band connector supporting transmission in the operating band.
[P2133] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-C is defined in TS 38.104 [2], clause 6.6.5.3.
[P2134] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-H is defined in TS 38.104 [2], clause 6.6.5.4.
[P2135] 6.6.5.3	Test purpose
[P2136] This test measures conducted spurious emissions while the transmitter is in operation.
[P2137] 6.6.5.4	Method of test
[P2138] 6.6.5.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2139] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2140] RF channels to be tested for single carrier:
[P2141] -	B when testing the spurious emissions below FDL_low - ΔfOBUE,
[P2142] -	T when testing the spurious emissions above FDL_high + ΔfOBUE; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2143] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier and/or CA:
[P2144] -	BRFBW when testing the spurious frequencies below FDL_low - ΔfOBUE; TRFBW when testing the spurious frequencies above FDL_high + ΔfOBUE in single-band operation; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2145] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW when testing the spurious frequencies below FDL_low - ΔfOBUE of the lowest operating band; B'RFBW_TRFBW when testing the spurious frequencies above FDL_high + ΔfOBUE of the highest operating band in multi-band operation, see clause 4.9.1.
[P2146] 6.6.5.4.2	Procedure
[P2147] For BS type 1-H where there may be multiple TAB connectors, they may be tested one at a time or multiple TAB connectors may be tested in parallel as shown in annex D.3.1. Whichever method is used the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested.
[P2148] 1)	Connect the single-band connector or multi-band connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.1.1 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.3.1 for BS type 1-H. All connectors not under test shall be terminated.
[P2149] 2)	Measurements shall use a measurement bandwidth in accordance to the conditions in clause 6.6.5.5.
[P2150] The measurement device characteristics shall be:
[P2151] -	Detection mode: True RMS.
[P2152] The emission power should be averaged over an appropriate time duration to ensure the measurement is within the measurement uncertainty in Table 4.1.2.2-1.
[P2153] 3)	For a connectors declared to be capable of single carrier operation only (D.16), set the representative connectors under test to transmit according to the applicable test configuration in clause 4.8 at rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC, or Prated,c,TABC, D.21). Channel set-up shall be according to NR-FR1-TM 1.1.
[P2154] For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2.
[P2155] For a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41), test shall be performed using N-TM according to clause 4.9.2.2.9.
[P2156] 4)	Measure the emission at the specified frequencies with specified measurement bandwidth.
[P2157] In addition, for multi-band connectors, the following steps shall apply:
[P2158] 5)	For a multi-band connectors and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved operating band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other operating band.

The application of either Category A or Category B limits shall be the same as for operating band unwanted emissions in clause 6.6.4, and as declared by the manufacturer (D.4).

[P2167] 6.6.5.5.1.2	Protection of the BS receiver of own or different BS
[P2168] This requirement shall be applied for NR FDD operation in order to prevent the receivers of the BSs being desensitised by emissions from a BS transmitter. It is measured at the transmit antenna connector for BS type 1-C or at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H for any type of BS which has common or separate Tx/Rx antenna connectors / TAB connectors.
[P2169] The basic limits are provided in table 6.6.5.5.1.2-1.

[P2172] 6.6.5.5.1.3	Additional spurious emissions requirements
[P2173] These requirements may be applied for the protection of system operating in frequency ranges other than the BS downlink operating band. The limits may apply as an optional protection of such systems that are deployed in the same geographical area as the BS, or they may be set by local or regional regulation as a mandatory requirement for an NR operating band. It is in some cases not stated in the present document whether a requirement is mandatory or under what exact circumstances that a limit applies, since this is set by local or regional regulation. An overview of regional requirements in the present document is given in clause 4.4.
[P2174] Some requirements may apply for the protection of specific equipment (UE, MS and/or BS) or equipment operating in specific systems (GSM, CDMA, UTRA, E-UTRA, NR, etc.) as listed below.
[P2175] The power of any spurious emission shall not exceed the basic limits of table 6.6.5.5.1.3-1 for a BS where requirements for co-existence with the system listed in the first column apply. For a multi-band connector, the exclusions and conditions in the Note column of table 6.6.5.5.1.3-1 apply for each supported operating band.

Emission limits for this excluded frequency range may be covered by local or regional requirements.
[P2179] NOTE 2:	Table 6.6.5.5.1.3-1 assumes that two operating bands, where the frequency ranges in TS 38.104 [2], table 5.2-1 would be overlapping, are not deployed in the same geographical area. For such a case of operation with overlapping frequency arrangements in the same geographical area, special co-existence requirements may apply that are not covered by the 3GPP specifications.
[P2180] NOTE 3:	TDD base stations deployed in the same geographical area, that are synchronized and use the same or adjacent operating bands can transmit without additional co-existence requirements. For unsynchronized base stations, special co-existence requirements may apply that are not covered by the 3GPP specifications.

[P2182] NOTE 5:	For NR Band n29 BS, specific solutions may be required to fulfil the spurious emissions limits for NR BS for co-existence with UTRA Band XII, E-UTRA Band 12 or NR Band n12 UL operating band, E-UTRA Band 17 UL operating band or E-UTRA Band 85 UL or NR Band n85 UL operating band.

[P2231] 6.6.5.5.1.4	Co-location with other base stations
[P2232] These requirements may be applied for the protection of other BS receivers when GSM900, DCS1800, PCS1900, GSM850, CDMA850, UTRA FDD, UTRA TDD, E-UTRA and/or NR BS are co-located with a BS.
[P2233] The requirements assume a 30 dB coupling loss between transmitter and receiver and are based on co-location with base stations of the same class.
[P2234] The basic limits are in table 6.6.5.5.1.4-1 for a BS where requirements for co-location with a BS type listed in the first column apply, depending on the declared BS class (D.2). For a multi-band connector, the exclusions and conditions in the Note column of table 6.6.5.5.1.4-1 shall apply for each supported operating band.
[P2235] Table 6.6.5.5.1.4-1: BS spurious emissions basic limits for BS co-located with another BS
[P2237] NOTE 1:	As defined in the scope for spurious emissions in this clause, the co-location requirements in table 6.6.5.5.1.4-1 do not apply for the frequency range extending ΔfOBUE immediately outside the BS transmit frequency range of a downlink operating band (see TS 38.104 [2] table 5.2-1). The current state-of-the-art technology does not allow a single generic solution for co-location with other system on adjacent frequencies for 30dB BS-BS minimum coupling loss. However, there are certain site-engineering solutions that can be used. These techniques are addressed in TR 25.942 [15].
[P2238] NOTE 2:	Table 6.6.5.5.1.4-1 assumes that two operating bands, where the corresponding BS transmit and receive frequency ranges in TS 38.104 [2] table 5.2-1 would be overlapping, are not deployed in the same geographical area. For such a case of operation with overlapping frequency arrangements in the same geographical area, special co-location requirements may apply that are not covered by the 3GPP specifications.
[P2239] NOTE 3:	Co-located TDD base stations that are synchronized and using the same or adjacent operating band can transmit without special co-locations requirements. For unsynchronized base stations, special co-location requirements may apply that are not covered by the 3GPP specifications.
[P2240] 6.6.5.5.2	(void)
[P2241] 6.6.5.5.3	BS type 1-C
[P2242] The Tx spurious emissions for BS type 1-C for each antenna connector shall not exceed the basic limits specified in clause 6.6.5.5.1.
[P2243] For Band n41 and n90 operation in Japan, the sum of the spurious emissions over all antenna connectors for BS type 1-C shall not exceed the basic limits defined in clause 6.6.5.5.1.
[P2244] 6.6.5.5.4	BS type 1-H
[P2245] The Tx spurious emissions requirements for BS type 1-H are that for each TAB connector TX min cell group and each applicable basic limit in clause 6.6.5.5.1, the power summation emissions at the TAB connectors of the TAB connector TX min cell group shall not exceed a limit specified as the basic limit + X, where X = 10log10(NTXU,countedpercell), unless stated differently in regional regulation.
[P2246] NOTE:	Conformance to the BS type 1-H spurious emission requirement can be demonstrated by meeting at least one of the following criteria as determined by the manufacturer:
[P2247] 1) The sum of the emissions power measured on each TAB connector in the TAB connector TX min cell group shall be less than or equal to the limit as defined in this clause for the respective frequency span.
[P2248] Or
[P2249] 2) The unwanted emissions power at each TAB connector shall be less than or equal to the BS type 1-H limit as defined in this clause for the respective frequency span, scaled by -10log10(n), where n is the number of TAB connectors in the TAB connector TX min cell group.
[P2250] 6.7	Transmitter intermodulation
[P2251] 6.7.1	Definition and applicability
[P2252] The transmitter intermodulation requirement is a measure of the capability of the transmitter unit to inhibit the generation of signals in its non-linear elements caused by presence of the wanted signal and an interfering signal reaching the transmitter unit via the antenna, RDN and antenna array. The requirement shall apply during the transmitter ON period and the transmitter transient period.
[P2253] For BS type 1-C, the transmitter intermodulation level is the power of the intermodulation products when an interfering signal is injected into the antenna connector.
[P2254] For BS type 1-H, the transmitter intermodulation level is the power of the intermodulation products when an interfering signal is injected into the TAB connector.
[P2255] For BS type 1-H, there are two types of transmitter intermodulation cases captured by the transmitter intermodulation requirement:
[P2256] 1)	Co-location transmitter intermodulation in which the interfering signal is from a co-located base station.
[P2257] 2)	Intra-system transmitter intermodulation in which the interfering signal is from other transmitter units within the BS type 1-H.
[P2258] For BS type 1-H, the co-location transmitter intermodulation requirement is considered sufficient if the interference signal for the co-location requirement is higher than the declared interference signal for intra-system transmitter (D.30) intermodulation requirement.
[P2259] 6.7.2	Minimum requirement
[P2260] The minimum requirement applies per single-band connector, or per multi-band connector supporting transmission in the operating band.
[P2261] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-C is defined in TS 38.104 [2], clause 6.7.2.
[P2262] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-H is defined in TS 38.104 [2], clause 6.7.3.
[P2263] 6.7.3	Test purpose
[P2264] The test purpose is to verify the ability of the transmitter units associated with the single-band connectors or multi-band connector under test to restrict the generation of intermodulation products in its nonlinear elements caused by presence of the wanted signal and an interfering signal reaching the transmitter via the antenna to below specified levels.
[P2265] 6.7.4	Method of test
[P2266] 6.7.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2267] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2268] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2269] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier and/or CA:
[P2270] -	MRFBW in single-band operation; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2271] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW in multi-band operation, see clause 4.9.1.
[P2272] NOTE:	When testing in M (or MRFBW), if the interferer is fully or partially located outside the supported frequency range, then the test shall be done instead in B (or BRFBW) and T (or TRFBW), and only with the interferer located inside the supported frequency range.
[P2273] 6.7.4.2	Procedure
[P2274] For BS type 1-H where there may be multiple TAB connectors, they may be tested one at a time or multiple TAB connectors may be tested in parallel as shown in annex D.3.1. Whichever method is used the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested.
[P2275] 1)	Connect the single-band connector or multi-band connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.1.2 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.3.2 for BS type 1-H. All connectors not under test shall be terminated.
[P2276] 2)	The measurement device characteristics shall be:
[P2277] -	Detection mode: True RMS.
[P2278] The emission power should be averaged over an appropriate time duration to ensure the measurement is within the measurement uncertainty in Table 4.1.2.2-1.
[P2279] 3)	For a connectors declared to be capable of single carrier operation only (D.16), set the representative connectors under test to transmit according to the applicable test configuration in clause 4.8 at rated carrier output power Prated,c,AC for BS type 1-C and Prated,c,TABC for BS type 1-H (D.21). Channel set-up shall be according to NR-FR1-TM 1.1.
[P2280] For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2.
[P2281] For a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41), test shall be performed using N-TM according to clause 4.9.2.2.9.
[P2282] 4)	Generate the interfering signal according to NR-FR1-TM-1.1, as defined in clause 4.9.2, with the minimum channel bandwidth (BWChannel) with 15 kHz SCS of the band defined in clause 5.3.5 of TS 38.104 [2] and a centre frequency offset from the lower/upper edge of the wanted signal or edge of sub-block inside a sub-block gap , for n = 1, 2 and 3, but exclude interfering frequencies that are outside of the allocated downlink operating band or interfering frequencies that are not completely within the sub-block gap or within the Inter RF Bandwidth gap.
[P2283] 5)	Adjust ATT attenuator (as in the test setup in annex D.1.2 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.3.2 for BS type 1-H) so that level of the interfering signal is as defined in clause 6.7.5.
[P2284] 6)	Perform the unwanted emission tests specified in clauses 6.6.3 and 6.6.4 for all third and fifth order intermodulation products which appear in the frequency ranges defined in clauses 6.6.3 and 6.6.4. The width of the intermodulation products shall be taken into account.
[P2285] 7)	Perform the transmitter spurious emissions test as specified in clause 6.6.5, for all third and fifth order intermodulation products which appear in the frequency ranges defined in clause 6.6.5. The width of the intermodulation products shall be taken into account.
[P2286] 8)	Verify that the emission level does not exceed the required level in clause 6.7.5 with the exception of interfering signal frequencies.
[P2287] 9)	Repeat the test for the remaining interfering signal centre frequency offsets according to step 4.
[P2288] 10)	Repeat the test for the remaining test signals defined in clause 6.7.5 for additional requirements and for BS type 1-H intra-system requirements.
[P2289] In addition, for multi-band connectors, the following steps shall apply:
[P2290] 11)	For a multi-band connectors and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved operating band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other operating band.
[P2291] NOTE:	The third order intermodulation products are centred at 2F1F2 and 2F2F1. The fifth order intermodulation products are centred at 3F12F2, 3F22F1, 4F1F2, and 4F2F1 where F1 represents the test signal centre frequency or centre frequency of each sub-block and F2 represents the interfering signal centre frequency. The widths of intermodulation products are:
[P2292] -	(n*BWF1 + m* BWF2) for the nF1mF2 products;
[P2293] -	(n* BWF2 + m* BWF1) for the nF2mF1 products;
[P2294] where BWF1 represents the test wanted signal RF bandwidth or channel bandwidth in case of single carrier, or sub-block bandwidth and BWF2 represents the interfering signal channel bandwidth.
[P2295] 6.7.5	Test requirements
[P2296] 6.7.5.1	BS type 1-C
[P2297] 6.7.5.1.1	Co-location minimum requirements
[P2298] For BS type 1-C, the wanted signal and interfering signal centre frequency is specified in table 6.7.5.1.1-1, where interfering signal level is rated total output power (Prated,t,AC) at antenna connector in the operating band – 30 dB.
[P2299] The requirement is applicable outside the Base Station RF Bandwidth or Radio Bandwidth. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges or Radio Bandwidth edges.
[P2300] For a BS operating in non-contiguous spectrum, the requirement is also applicable inside a sub-block gap for interfering signal offsets where the interfering signal falls completely within the sub-block gap. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges.
[P2301] For a multi-band connector, the requirement shall apply relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges of each supported operating band. In case the Inter RF Bandwidth gap is less than 3*BWChannel MHz (where BWChannel is the minimal BS channel bandwidth of the band), the requirement in the gap shall apply only for interfering signal offsets where the interfering signal falls completely within the Inter RF Bandwidth gap.
[P2302] The transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.1.1-1.
[P2303] Table 6.7.5.1.1-1: Interfering and wanted signals for the co-location transmitter intermodulation requirement
[P2305] 6.7.5.1.2	Additional requirements
[P2306] For Band n41 and n90 operation in Japan, the sum of transmitter intermodulation level over all antenna connectors shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.1.2-1.
[P2307] Table 6.7.5.1.2-1: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n41 and n90
[P2309] For Band n26 and n28 operation in Japan, when the narrowest channel bandwidth supported by the BS is 5MHz or wider, the transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.1.2-2.
[P2310] Table 6.7.5.1.2-2: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n26 and n28 when the narrowest channel bandwidth supported by the BS is 5MHz or wider
[P2312] For Band n79 operation in Japan, the transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.1.2-3.
[P2313] Table 6.7.5.1.2-3: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n79
[P2315] 6.7.5.2	BS type 1-H
[P2316] 6.7.5.2.1	Co-location minimum requirements
[P2317] The transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.2.1-1.
[P2318] The requirement is applicable outside the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges or Radio Bandwidth edges.
[P2319] For TAB connectors supporting operation in non-contiguous spectrum, the requirement is also applicable inside a sub-block gap for interfering signal offsets where the interfering signal falls completely within the sub-block gap. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges.
[P2320] For multi-band connector, the requirement shall apply relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges of each operating band. In case the inter RF Bandwidth gap is less than 3*BWChannel MHz (where BWChannel is the minimal BS channel bandwidth of the band), the requirement in the gap shall apply only for interfering signal offsets where the interfering signal falls completely within the inter RF Bandwidth gap.
[P2321] Table 6.7.5.2.1-1: Interfering and wanted signals for the co-location transmitter intermodulation requirement
[P2323] 6.7.5.2.2	Intra-system minimum requirements
[P2324] The transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3 and 6.6.4 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.2.2-1.
[P2325] Table 6.7.5.2.2-1: Interfering and wanted signals for intra-system transmitter intermodulation requirement
[P2327] 6.7.5.2.3	Additional requirements
[P2328] For Band n41 operation in Japan, the transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.2.3-1.
[P2329] Table 6.7.5.2.3-1: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n41 and n90
[P2331] For Band n26 and n28 operation in Japan, when the narrowest channel bandwidth supported by the BS is 5 MHz or wider, the transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.2.3-2.
[P2332] Table 6.7.5.2.3-2: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n26 and n28 when the narrowest channel bandwidth supported by the BS is 5MHz or wider
[P2334] For Band n79 operation in Japan, the transmitter intermodulation level shall not exceed the unwanted emission limits in clauses 6.6.3, 6.6.4 and 6.6.5 in the presence of an NR interfering signal according to table 6.7.5.2.3-3.
[P2335] Table 6.7.5.2.3-3: Interfering and wanted signals for the additional transmitter intermodulation requirement for Band n79
[P2337] 7	Conducted receiver characteristics
[P2338] 7.1	General
[P2339] Conducted receiver characteristics are specified at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C and at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H, with full complement of transceivers for the configuration in normal operating condition.
[P2340] Unless otherwise stated, the following arrangements apply for conducted receiver characteristics requirements in clause 7:
[P2341] -	Requirements apply during the BS receive period.
[P2342] -	Requirements shall be met for any transmitter setting.
[P2343] -	For FDD operation the requirements shall be met with the transmitter unit(s) ON.
[P2344] -	Throughput requirements defined for the conducted receiver characteristics do not assume HARQ retransmissions.
[P2345] -	When BS is configured to receive multiple carriers, all the throughput requirements are applicable for each received carrier.
[P2346] -	For ACS, blocking and intermodulation characteristics, the negative offsets of the interfering signal apply relative to the lower Base Station RF Bandwidth edge or sub-block edge inside a sub-block gap, and the positive offsets of the interfering signal apply relative to the upper Base Station RF Bandwidth edge or sub-block edge inside a sub-block gap.
[P2347] -	Requirements shall also apply for BS supporting NB-IoT operation in NR in-band. The corresponding NB-IoT requirements are specified in clause 7 of TS 36.141 [24].
[P2348] NOTE 1:	In normal operating condition the BS in FDD operation is configured to transmit and receive at the same time.
[P2349] NOTE 2:	In normal operating condition the BS in TDD operation is configured to TX OFF power during receive period.
[P2350] For BS type 1-H if a number of TAB connectors have been declared equivalent (D.32), only a representative one is necessary to demonstrate conformance.
[P2351] In clause 7.6.5.3, if representative TAB connectors are used then per connector criteria (option 2) shall be applied.
[P2352] For BS type 1-H there is no requirement specified for band n46, n100, n101 and n102.
[P2354] 7.2	Reference sensitivity level
[P2355] 7.2.1	Definition and applicability
[P2356] The reference sensitivity power level PREFSENS is the minimum mean power received at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H at which a throughput requirement shall be met for a specified reference measurement channel.
[P2357] 7.2.2	Minimum requirement
[P2358] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-C is in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.2.2.
[P2359] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-H is in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.2.2.
[P2360] 7.2.3	Test purpose
[P2361] To verify that for the BS type 1-C receiver and each BS type 1-H TAB connector at the reference sensitivity level the throughput requirement shall be met for a specified reference measurement channel.
[P2362] 7.2.4	Method of test
[P2363] 7.2.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2364] Test environment:
[P2365] -	Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2366] -	Extreme, see annexes B.3 and B.5.
[P2367] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: B, M and T; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2368] Under extreme test environment, the test shall be performed on each of B, M and T under extreme power supply conditions as defined in annex B.5.
[P2369] NOTE:	Tests under extreme power supply conditions also test extreme temperatures.
[P2370] 7.2.4.2	Procedure
[P2371] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2372] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2373] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.1 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.1 for BS type 1-H.
[P2374] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit a signal using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2, for BS type 1-C set the antenna connector to the manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21).
[P2375] 3)	Start the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit the Fixed Reference Channels for reference sensitivity according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2376] 4)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal power as specified in clause 7.2.5.
[P2377] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2378] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2379] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2380] 7.2.5	Test requirements
[P2381] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.1 with parameters specified in table 7.2.5-1 for Wide Area BS, in table 7.2.5-2 for Medium Range BS and in table 7.2.5-3 for Local Area BS in any operating band except for band n46, n96, n102, and n104.
[P2382] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.2.5-2a for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.5-3a for Local Area BS, for band n46.
[P2383] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.2.5-2b for Medium Range BS and in table 7.2.5-3b for Local Area BS, for band n96 and n102.
[P2384] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.1 with parameters specified in table 7.2.5-1a for Wide Area BS, in table 7.2.5-2c for Medium Range BS and in table 7.2.5-3c for Local Area BS for band n104.
[P2385] The reference sensitivity level requirements for NB-IoT are specified in clause 7.2.5 of TS 36.141 [24].
[P2386] Table 7.2.5-1: NR Wide Area BS reference sensitivity levels
[P2388] Table 7.2.5-1a: NR Wide Area BS reference sensitivity levels for band n104
[P2390] Table 7.2.5-2: NR Medium Range BS reference sensitivity levels
[P2392] Table 7.2.5-2a: NR Medium Range BS reference sensitivity levels for band n46
[P2394] Table 7.2.5-2b: NR Medium Range BS reference sensitivity levels for band n96 and n102
[P2396] Table 7.2.5-2c: NR Medium Range BS reference sensitivity levels for band n104
[P2398] Table 7.2.5-3: NR Local Area BS reference sensitivity levels
[P2400] Table 7.2.5-3a: NR Local Area BS reference sensitivity levels for band n46
[P2402] Table 7.2.5-3b: NR Local Area BS reference sensitivity levels for band n96 and n102
[P2404] Table 7.2.5-3c: NR Local Area BS reference sensitivity levels for band n104
[P2406] 7.3	Dynamic range
[P2407] 7.3.1	Definition and applicability
[P2408] The dynamic range is specified as a measure of the capability of the receiver to receive a wanted signal in the presence of an interfering signal at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H inside the received BS channel bandwidth. In this condition, a throughput requirement shall be met for a specified reference measurement channel. The interfering signal for the dynamic range requirement is an AWGN signal.
[P2409] 7.3.2	Minimum requirement
[P2410] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-C is in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.3.2.
[P2411] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-H is in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.3.2.
[P2412] 7.3.3	Test purpose
[P2413] To verify that the BS type 1-C receiver and each BS type 1-H TAB connector receiver dynamic range, the relative throughput shall fulfil the specified limit.
[P2414] 7.3.4	Method of test
[P2415] 7.3.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2416] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2417] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2418] 7.3.4.2	Procedure
[P2419] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2420] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2421] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.2 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.2 for BS type 1-H.
[P2422] 2)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.3.5-1 to table 7.3.5-3 according to the appropriate BS class, as well as table 7.3.5-1a to table 7.3.5-3a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2423] 3)	Set the Signal generator for the AWGN interfering signal at the same frequency as the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.3.5-1 to table 7.3.5-3 according to the appropriate BS class, as well as table 7.3.5-1a to table 7.3.5-3a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2424] 4)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.2, as well as annex A.15 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2425] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2426] 5)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2427] 7.3.5	Test requirements
[P2428] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.3.2-1 for Wide Area BS, in table 7.3.2-2 for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.2-3 for Local Area BS in any operating band except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104.
[P2429] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.3.5-2b for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.5-3b for Local Area BS, for band n46.
[P2430] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.3.5-2c for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.5-3c for Local Area BS, for band n96 and n102.
[P2431] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.2 with parameters specified in table 7.3.5-1b for Wide Area BS, in table 7.3.5-2d for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.5-3d for Local Area BS in band n104.
[P2432] For NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in Annex A of TS 36.141 [24] with parameters specified in table 7.3.5-1a for Wide Area BS, in table 7.3.5-2a for Medium Range BS and in table 7.3.5-3a for Local Area BS.

[P2435] Table 7.3.5-1a: Wide Area BS dynamic range for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band

[P2441] Table 7.3.5-2a: Medium Range BS dynamic range for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band

[P2445] Table 7.3.5-2c: Medium Range BS dynamic range for band n96 and n102
