[P2448] Table 7.3.5-3: Local Area BS dynamic range
[P2450] Table 7.3.5-3a: Local Area BS dynamic range for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band
[P2452] Table 7.3.5-3b: Local Area BS dynamic range for band n46
[P2454] Table 7.3.5-3c: Local area BS dynamic range for band n96 and n102
[P2456] Table 7.3.5-3d: Local Area BS dynamic range for n104
[P2458] 7.4	In-band selectivity and blocking
[P2459] 7.4.1	Adjacent Channel Selectivity (ACS)
[P2460] 7.4.1.1	Definition and applicability
[P2461] Adjacent channel selectivity (ACS) is a measure of the receiver's ability to receive a wanted signal at its assigned channel frequency at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H in the presence of an adjacent channel signal with a specified centre frequency offset of the interfering signal to the band edge of a victim system.
[P2462] 7.4.1.2	Minimum requirement
[P2463] The minimum requirement for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.4.1.2.
[P2464] 7.4.1.3	Test purpose
[P2465] The test purpose is to verify the ability of the BS receiver filter to suppress interfering signals in the channels adjacent to the wanted channel.
[P2466] 7.4.1.4	Method of test
[P2467] 7.4.1.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2468] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2469] RF channels to be tested for single carrier (SC): M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2470] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier (MC):
[P2471] -	MRFBW for single-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1,
[P2472] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW for multi-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1.
[P2473] 7.4.1.4.2	Procedure
[P2474] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2475] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2476] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.3 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.3 for BS type 1-H.
[P2477] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit:
[P2478] -	For single carrier operation set the connector under test to transmit at manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21).
[P2479] -	For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2
[P2480] 3)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.4.1.5-1.
[P2481] 4)	Set the signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified in table 7.4.1.5-1 and 7.4.1.5-2.
[P2482] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2483] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2484] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2485] 7.4.1.5	Test requirements
[P2486] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel.
[P2487] For BS operating except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104, the wanted and the interfering signal coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector are specified in table 7.4.1.5-1 and the frequency offset between the wanted and interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2 for ACS. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in table 7.2.5-1, 7.2.5-2 and 7.2.5-3 for each channel bandwidth in any operating band except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104 and further specified in annex A.1. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2488] For BS operating in band n46, n96 and n102, the wanted and the interfering signal coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector are specified in table 7.4.1.5-1a,7.4.1.5-1b and the frequency offset between the wanted and interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2a for ACS. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in table 7.2.5-2a,7.2.5-3a and 7.2.5-3b for each BS channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1a. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex D.
[P2489] For BS operating in band n104, the wanted and the interfering signal coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector are specified in table 7.4.1.5-1b and the frequency offset between the wanted and interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2 for ACS. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in table 7.2.5-1a, 7.2.5-2c, and 7.2.5-3c for each BS channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex D.
[P2490] For BS supporting NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the wanted and the interfering signal coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector are specified in table 7.4.1.5-1 and the frequency offset between the wanted and interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2 for ACS. The reference measurement channel for the NB-IoT wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.5 of TS 36.141 [24]. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2491] The ACS requirement is applicable outside the Base Station RF Bandwidth or Radio Bandwidth. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base station RF Bandwidth edges or Radio Bandwidth edges.
[P2492] For a BS operating in non-contiguous spectrum within any operating band, the ACS requirement shall apply in addition inside any sub-block gap, in case the sub-block gap size is at least as wide as the NR interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges inside the sub-block gap.
[P2493] For a multi-band connector, the ACS requirement shall apply in addition inside any Inter RF Bandwidth gap, in case the Inter RF Bandwidth gap size is at least as wide as the NR interfering signal in table 7.4.1.5-2. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges inside the Inter RF Bandwidth gap
[P2494] Conducted requirement is defined at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C and at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H.
[P2495] Table 7.4.1.5-1: Base station ACS requirement
[P2497] Table 7.4.1.5-1a: Base station ACS requirement for band n46, n96 and n102
[P2499] Table 7.4.1.5-1b: Base station ACS requirement for band n104
[P2501] Table 7.4.1.5-2: Base Station ACS interferer frequency offset values
[P2503] Table 7.4.1.5-2a: Base Station ACS interferer frequency offset values for band n46, n96 and n102
[P2505] 7.4.2	In-band blocking
[P2506] 7.4.2.1	Definition and applicability
[P2507] The in-band blocking characteristics is a measure of the receiver's ability to receive a wanted signal at its assigned channel at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H in the presence of an unwanted interferer, which is an NR signal for general blocking or an NR signal with one resource block for narrowband blocking.
[P2508] 7.4.2.2	Minimum requirement
[P2509] The minimum requirements for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.4.2.2.
[P2510] 7.4.2.3	Test purpose
[P2511] The test purpose is to verify the ability of the BS receiver to withstand high-levels of in-band interference from unwanted signals at specified frequency offsets without undue degradation of its sensitivity.
[P2512] 7.4.2.4	Method of test
[P2513] 7.4.2.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2514] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2515] RF channels to be tested for single carrier (SC): M; see clause 4.9.1
[P2516] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier (MC) and/or CA:
[P2517] -	MRFBW for single-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1,
[P2518] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW for multi-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1.
[P2519] NOTE:	When testing in M (or MRFBW), if the interferer is fully or partially located outside the supported frequency range, then the test shall be done instead in B (or BRFBW) and T (or TRFBW), and only with the interferer located inside the supported frequency range.
[P2520] 7.4.2.4.2	Procedure for general blocking
[P2521] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2522] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2523] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.3 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.3 for BS type 1-H.
[P2524] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit:
[P2525] -	For single carrier operation set the connector under test to transmit at manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21).
[P2526] -	For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2.
[P2527] 3)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.4.2.5-1.
[P2528] 4)	Set the signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified in table 7.4.2.5-1. The interfering signal shall be swept with a step size of 1 MHz starting from the minimum offset to the channel edges of the wanted signals as specified in table 7.4.2.5-1.
[P2529] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2530] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2531] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2532] 7.4.2.4.3	Procedure for narrowband blocking
[P2533] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2534] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2535] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.3 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.3 for BS type 1-H.
[P2536] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit:
[P2537] -	For single carrier operation set the connector under test to transmit at manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21).
[P2538] -	For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2.
[P2539] 3)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.4.2.5-2, as well as table 7.4.2.5-2a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2540] 4)	Set the signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified in table 7.4.2.5-2 and 7.4.2.5-3, as well as table 7.4.2.5-2a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41). Set-up and sweep the interfering RB centre frequency offset to the channel edge of the wanted signal according to table 7.4.2.5-3.
[P2541] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2542] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2543] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2544] 7.4.2.5	Test requirements
[P2545] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector using the parameters in tables 7.4.2.5-1, 7.4.2.5-2 and 7.4.2.5-3 for general blocking and narrowband blocking requirements. Narrowband blocking requirements are not applied for band n46, n96, n102 and n104. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.5 for each channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2546] For NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector using the parameters in tables 7.4.2.5-1, 7.4.2.5-2a and 7.4.2.5-3 for general blocking and narrowband blocking requirements. The reference measurement channel for the NB-IoT wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.5 of TS 36.141 [24]. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2547] The in-band blocking requirements apply outside the Base Station RF Bandwidth or Radio Bandwidth. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges or Radio Bandwidth edges.
[P2548] For BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H, the in-band blocking requirement applies from FUL_low - ΔfOOB to FUL_high + ΔfOOB, excluding the downlink frequency range of the operating band. The ΔfOOB for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H is defined in table 7.4.2.5-0.
[P2549] Minimum conducted requirement is defined at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C and at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H.
[P2550] Table 7.4.2.5-0: ΔfOOB offset for NR operating bands
[P2552] For band n46, n96 and n102, ΔfOOB is defined in table 7.4.2.5-0a.
[P2553] Table 7.4.2.5-0a: ΔfOOB offset for NR operating bands
[P2554] For band n104, ΔfOOB for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H is defined in table 7.4.2.5-0b.
[P2555] Table 7.4.2.5-0b: ΔfOOB offset for NR operating bands for band n104
[P2557] For a BS operating in non-contiguous spectrum within any operating band, the in-band blocking requirements apply in addition inside any sub-block gap, in case the sub-block gap size is at least as wide as twice the interfering signal minimum offset in table 7.4.2.5-1. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges inside the sub-block gap.
[P2558] For a multi-band connector, the blocking requirements apply in the in-band blocking frequency ranges for each supported operating band. The requirement applies in addition inside any Inter RF Bandwidth gap, in case the Inter RF Bandwidth gap size is at least as wide as twice the interfering signal minimum offset in table 7.4.2.5-1.
[P2559] For a BS operating in non-contiguous spectrum within any operating band, the narrowband blocking requirement applies in addition inside any sub-block gap, in case the sub-block gap size is at least as wide as the channel bandwidth of the NR interfering signal in table 7.4.2.5-3. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges inside the sub-block gap.
[P2560] For a multi-band connector, the narrowband blocking requirement applies in addition inside any Inter RF Bandwidth gap, in case the Inter RF Bandwidth gap size is at least as wide as the NR interfering signal in table 7.4.2.5-3. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges inside the Inter RF Bandwidth gap.
[P2561] Table 7.4.2.5-1: Base station general blocking requirement
[P2563] Table 7.4.2.5-1a: Base station general blocking requirement for n46
[P2565] Table 7.4.2.5-1b: Base station general blocking requirement for n96 and n102
[P2567] Table 7.4.2.5-2: Base station narrowband blocking requirement
[P2569] Table 7.4.2.5-2a: Base Station narrowband blocking requirement for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band
[P2571] Table 7.4.2.5-3: Base station narrowband blocking interferer frequency offsets
[P2573] 7.4.2.5.1	Additional narrowband blocking requirement for Band n100
[P2574] The following requirement shall apply to BS operating in Band n100 in CEPT countries. For the wanted and interfering signal coupled to the antenna connector, using the parameters in table 7.4.2.5.1-1 and 7.4.2.5.1-2, the throughput shall be ≥ 95 % of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel.
[P2576] Table 7.4.2.5.1-1: Additional narrowband blocking requirement for n100
[P2578] Table 7.4.2.5.1-2: Interferer frequency for additional narrowband blocking requirement for n100
[P2580] 7.5	Out-of-band blocking
[P2581] 7.5.1	Definition and applicability
[P2582] The out-of-band blocking characteristics is a measure of the receiver ability to receive a wanted signal at its assigned channel at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H in the presence of an unwanted interferer out of the operating band, which is a CW signal for out-of-band blocking.
[P2583] 7.5.2	Minimum requirement
[P2584] The minimum requirements for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.5.2.
[P2585] 7.5.3	Test purpose
[P2586] To verify that the BS type 1-C receiver and each BS type 1-H TAB connector receiver dynamic range, the relative throughput shall fulfil the specified limit.
[P2587] 7.5.4	Method of test
[P2588] 7.5.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2589] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2590] RF channels to be tested for single carrier (SC):
[P2591] -	M; see clause 4.9.1
[P2592] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier (MC):
[P2593] -	MRFBW for single-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1,
[P2594] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW for multi-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1.
[P2595] In addition, for a multi-band connector:
[P2596] -	For BRFBW_T'RFBW, out-of-band blocking testing above the highest operating band may be omitted.
[P2597] -	For B'RFBW_TRFBW, out-of-band blocking testing below the lowest operating band may be omitted.
[P2598] 7.5.4.2	Procedure
[P2599] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2600] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2601] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.5 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.3 for BS type 1-H.
[P2602] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit a signal according to clause 4.9.2, connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8.
[P2603] The transmitter may be turned off for the out-of-band blocker tests when the frequency of the blocker is such that no IM2 or IM3 products fall inside the bandwidth of the wanted signal.
[P2604] 3)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal as defined in clause 7.5.5 to transmit as specified in table 7.5.5.1-1 and 7.5.5.2-1.
[P2605] 4)	Set the Signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified in table 7.5.5.1-1 and 7.5.5.2-1. The CW interfering signal shall be swept with a step size of 1 MHz over than range 1 MHz to (FUL_low - ΔfOOB) MHz and (FUL_high + ΔfOOB) MHz to 12750 MHz.
[P2606] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2607] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2608] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2609] 7.5.5	Test requirements
[P2610] 7.5.5.1	General requirements
[P2611] Except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector using the parameters in table 7.5.5.1-1. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.2 for each channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1.
[P2612] For band n46, n96 and n102, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector using the parameters in table 7.5.5.1-1a.
[P2613] For BS type 1-C operating in band n104, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector using the parameters in table 7.5.5.1-1a.
[P2614] For BS type 1-H operating in band n104, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-H TAB connector using the parameters in table 7.5.5.1-1.
[P2615] For NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS type 1-C antenna connector using the parameters in table 7.5.5.1-1. The reference measurement channel for the NB-IoT wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.5 of TS 36.141 [24]. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2616] For BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H the out-of-band blocking requirement apply from 1 MHz to FUL_low - ΔfOOB and from FUL_high + ΔfOOB up to 12750 MHz, including the downlink frequency range of the FDD operating band for BS supporting FDD. The ΔfOOB for or BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H is defined in table 7.4.2.5-0.
[P2617] Minimum conducted requirement is defined at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C and at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H.
[P2618] For a BS capable of multi-band operation, the requirement in the out-of-band blocking frequency ranges apply for each operating band, with the exception that the in-band blocking frequency ranges of all supported operating bands according to clause 7.4.2.5 shall be excluded from the out-of-band blocking requirement.
[P2619] Table 7.5.5.1-1: Out-of-band blocking performance requirement
[P2621] Table 7.5.5.1-1a: Out-of-band blocking performance requirement for NR band n46, n96, n102 and n104 for BS type 1-C
[P2623] 7.5.5.2	Co-location requirements
[P2624] This additional blocking requirement may be applied for the protection of NR BS receivers when GSM, CDMA, UTRA, E-UTRA BS or NR BS operating in a different frequency band are co-located with a NR BS. The requirement is applicable to all channel bandwidths supported by the NR BS.
[P2625] The requirements in this clause assume a 30 dB coupling loss between interfering transmitter and NR BS receiver and are based on co-location with base stations of the same class.
[P2626] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted and an interfering signal coupled to BS antenna input using the parameters in table 7.5.5.2-1 for all the BS classes. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in tables 7.2.5-1, 7.2.5-2 and 7.2.5-3 for each channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1.
[P2627] For BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H blocking requirement for co-location with BS in other bands is applied for all operating bands for which co-location protection is provided.
[P2628] Minimum conducted requirement is defined at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C and at the TAB connector for BS type 1-H.
[P2629] Table 7.5.5.2-1: Blocking performance requirement for NR BS when co-located with BS in other frequency bands.
[P2631] 7.5.5.3	Additional requirement
[P2632] 7.5.5.3.1	Additional requirement for Band n101
[P2633] The following requirement may apply to BS operating in Band n101 in CEPT countries. For the wanted and interfering signal coupled to the antenna connector, using the parameters in table 7.5.5.3.1-1, the throughput shall be ≥ 95 % of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel.
[P2635] Table 7.5.5.3.1-1: Additional out-of-band blocking requirement for n101
[P2637] 7.6	Receiver spurious emissions
[P2638] 7.6.1	Definition and applicability
[P2639] The receiver spurious emissions power is the power of emissions generated or amplified in a receiver unit that appear at the antenna connector (for BS type 1-C) or at the TAB connector (for BS type 1-H). The requirements apply to all BS with separate RX and TX antenna connectors / TAB connectors.
[P2640] NOTE:	In this case for FDD operation the test is performed when both TX and RX are ON, with the TX antenna connectors / TAB connectors terminated.
[P2641] For antenna connectors / TAB connectors supporting both RX and TX in TDD, the requirements apply during the transmitter OFF period. For antenna connectors / TAB connectors supporting both RX and TX in FDD, the RX spurious emissions requirements are superseded by the TX spurious emissions requirements, as specified in clause 6.6.5.
[P2642] For RX-only multi-band connectors, the spurious emissions requirements are subject to exclusion zones in each supported operating band. For multi-band connectors that both transmit and receive in operating band supporting TDD, RX spurious emissions requirements are applicable during the TX OFF period, and are subject to exclusion zones in each supported operating band.
[P2643] For BS type 1-H manufacturer shall declare TAB connector RX min cell groups (D.33). Every TAB connector of BS type 1-H supporting reception in an operating band shall map to one TAB connector RX min cell group, where mapping of TAB connectors to cells/beams is implementation dependent.
[P2644] The number of active receiver units that are considered when calculating the conducted RX spurious emission limits (NRXU,counted) for BS type 1-H is calculated as follows:
[P2645] NRXU,counted = min(NRXU,active , 8 × Ncells)
[P2646] NRXU,countedpercell is used for scaling of basic limits and is derived as NRXU,countedpercell = NRXU,counted / Ncells, where Ncells is defined in clause 6.1.
[P2647] NOTE:	NRXU,active is the number of actually active receiver units and is independent to the declaration of Ncells.
[P2648] 7.6.2	Minimum requirement
[P2649] The minimum requirements for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.6.2.
[P2650] 7.6.3	Test purpose
[P2651] The test purpose is to verify the ability of the BS to limit the interference caused by receiver spurious emissions to other systems.
[P2652] 7.6.4	Method of test
[P2653] 7.6.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2654] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2655] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2656] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier:
[P2657] -	MRFBW in single-band operation, see clause 4.9.1,
[P2658] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW in multi-band operation, see clause 4.9.1.
[P2659] 7.6.4.2	Procedure
[P2660] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test,
[P2661] For BS type 1-H where there may be multiple TAB connectors they may be tested one at a time or multiple TAB connectors may be tested in parallel as shown in annex D.4.4. Whichever method is used the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested.
[P2662] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.6 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.4 for BS type 1-H.
[P2663] 2)	For FDD operation, for separate RX only connectors with single carrier operation set the connector under test to transmit at manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21). Channel set-up shall be according to NR-FR1-TM 1.1.
[P2664] For FDD operation, for separate RX only connectors declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.
[P2665] For separate RX only connectors declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41), test shall be performed using N-TM according to clause 4.9.2.2.9.
[P2666] For TDD connectors capable of transmit and receive ensure the transmitter is OFF.
[P2667] 3)	Set the measurement equipment parameters as specified in table 7.6.5.1-1.
[P2668] The measurement device characteristics shall be:
[P2669] -	Detection mode: True RMS.
[P2670] The emission power should be averaged over an appropriate time duration to ensure the measurement is within the measurement uncertainty in Table 4.1.2.3-1.
[P2671] 4)	Measure the spurious emissions over each frequency range described in table 7.6.5.1-1.
[P2672] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2673] 5)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2674] 7.6.5	Test requirements
[P2675] 7.6.5.1	Basic limits
[P2676] The receiver spurious emissions limits are provided in table 7.6.5.1-1.
[P2677] Table 7.6.5.1-1: General BS receiver spurious emissions limits
[P2679] 7.6.5.2	BS type 1-C
[P2680] The RX spurious emissions requirements for BS type 1-C are that for each antenna connector, the power of emissions shall not exceed basic limits specified in table 7.6.5.1-1.
[P2681] For Band n41 and n90 operation in Japan, the sum of RX spurious emissions over all antenna connectors for BS type 1-C shall not exceed basic limits specified in table 7.6.5.1-1.The Rx spurious emissions requirements shall apply to BS that support NR or NR with NB-IoT operation in NR in-band.
[P2682] 7.6.5.3	BS type 1-H
[P2683] The RX spurious emissions requirements for BS type 1-H are that for each applicable basic limit specified in table 7.6.5.1-1 for each TAB connector RX min cell group, the power sum of emissions at respective TAB connectors shall not exceed the BS limits specified as the basic limits + X, where X = 10log10(NRXU,countedpercell), unless stated differently in regional regulation.
[P2684] The RX spurious emission requirements are applied per the TAB connector RX min cell group for all the configurations supported by the BS.
[P2685] NOTE:	Conformance to the BS receiver spurious emissions requirement can be demonstrated by meeting at least one of the following criteria as determined by the manufacturer:
[P2686] 1)	The sum of the spurious emissions power measured on each TAB connector in the TAB connector RX min cell group shall be less than or equal to the BS limit above for the respective frequency span.
[P2687] Or
[P2688] 2)	The spurious emissions power at each TAB connector shall be less than or equal to the BS limit as defined above for the respective frequency span, scaled by -10log10(n), where n is the number of TAB connectors in the TAB connector RX min cell group.
[P2689] 7.7	Receiver intermodulation
[P2690] 7.7.1	Definition and applicability
[P2691] Third and higher order mixing of the two interfering RF signals can produce an interfering signal in the band of the desired channel. Intermodulation response rejection is a measure of the capability of the receiver to receive a wanted signal on its assigned channel frequency at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H in the presence of two interfering signals which have a specific frequency relationship to the wanted signal.
[P2692] 7.7.2	Minimum requirement
[P2693] The minimum requirements for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.7.2.
[P2694] 7.7.3	Test purpose
[P2696] The test purpose is to verify the ability of the BS receiver to inhibit the generation of intermodulation products in its non-linear elements caused by the presence of two high-level interfering signals at frequencies with a specific relationship to the frequency of the wanted signal.
[P2697] 7.7.4	Method of test
[P2698] 7.7.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2699] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2700] RF channels to be tested for single carrier (SC): M; see clause 4.9.1
[P2701] Base Station RF Bandwidth positions to be tested for multi-carrier (MC) and/or CA:
[P2702] -	MRFBW for single-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1,
[P2703] -	BRFBW_T'RFBW and B'RFBW_TRFBW for multi-band connector(s), see clause 4.9.1.
[P2704] NOTE:	When testing in M (or MRFBW), if the interferer is fully or partially located outside the supported frequency range, then the test shall be done instead in B (or BRFBW) and T (or TRFBW), and only with the interferer located inside the supported frequency range.
[P2705] 7.7.4.2	Procedure
[P2706] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2707] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2708] 1)	Connect the connector under test to measurement equipment as shown in annex D.2.7 for BS type 1-C and in annex D.4.6 for BS type 1-H.
[P2709] 2)	For FDD operation, set the BS to transmit:
[P2710] -	For single carrier operation set the connector under test to transmit at manufacturers declared rated carrier output power (Prated,c,AC or Prated,c,TABC, D.21).
[P2711] -	For a connector under test declared to be capable of multi-carrier and/or CA operation (D.15-D.16) set the connector under test to transmit on all carriers configured using the applicable test configuration and corresponding power setting specified in clauses 4.7 and 4.8 using the corresponding test models or set of physical channels in clause 4.9.2.
[P2712] 3)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified in table 7.7.5-1 and 7.7.5-3.
[P2713] 4)	Set the signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified in table 7.7.5-2 and 7.7.5-4.
[P2714] 5)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2715] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2716] 6)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2717] 7.7.5	Test requirements
[P2718] The throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted signal at the assigned channel frequency and two interfering signals coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector or BS type 1-H TAB connector, with the conditions specified in tables 7.7.5-1 and 7.7.5-2 for intermodulation performance in any operating band except for band n46, n96 and n102, and 7.7.5-1a for band n46, n96 and n102 and in tables 7.7.5-3, and 7.7.5-4 for narrowband intermodulation performance. Narrowband intermodulation requirements are not applied for band n46, n96, n102 and n104. The reference measurement channel for the wanted signal is identified in tables 7.2.5-1 to 7.2.5-3 for each channel bandwidth and further specified in annex A.1. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2719] For NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel, with a wanted signal at the assigned channel frequency and two interfering signals coupled to the BS type 1-C antenna connector, with the conditions specified in tables 7.7.5-1 and 7.7.5-2 for intermodulation performance and in tables 7.7.5-3, and 7.7.5-4 for narrowband intermodulation performance. The reference measurement channel for the NB-IoT wanted signal is identified in clause 7.2.5 of TS 36.141 [24]. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2720] The subcarrier spacing for the modulated interfering signal shall in general be the same as the subcarrier spacing for the wanted signal, except for the case of wanted signal subcarrier spacing 60 kHz and BS channel bandwidth <=20MHz, for which the subcarrier spacing of the interfering signal should be 30 kHz.
[P2721] The receiver intermodulation requirement is applicable outside the Base Station RF Bandwidth or Radio Bandwidth edges. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges or Radio Bandwidth edges.
[P2722] For a BS operating in non-contiguous spectrum within any operating band, the narrowband intermodulation requirement applies in addition inside any sub-block gap in case the sub-block gap is at least as wide as the channel bandwidth of the NR interfering signal in table 7.7.5-2 or 7.7.5-4. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the sub-block edges inside the sub-block gap.
[P2723] For a multi-band connectors, the intermodulation requirement applies in addition inside any Inter RF Bandwidth gap, in case the gap size is at least twice as wide as the NR interfering signal centre frequency offset from the Base Station RF Bandwidth edge.
[P2724] For a multi-band connectors, the narrowband intermodulation requirement applies in addition inside any Inter RF Bandwidth gap in case the gap size is at least as wide as the NR interfering signal in tables 7.7.5-2 and 7.7.5-4. The interfering signal offset is defined relative to the Base Station RF Bandwidth edges inside the Inter RF Bandwidth gap.
[P2725] Table 7.7.5-1: General intermodulation requirement
[P2727] Table 7.7.5-1a: General intermodulation requirement for band n46, n96 and n102
[P2730] Table 7.7.5-2: Interfering signals for intermodulation requirement
[P2732] Table 7.7.5-2a: Interfering signals for intermodulation requirement for band n46, n96 and n102
[P2734] Table 7.7.5-3: Narrowband intermodulation performance requirement in FR1
[P2736] Table 7.7.5-4: Interfering signals for narrowband intermodulation requirement in FR1
[P2738] 7.7.5.1	Additional narrowband intermodulation requirement for Band n100
[P2739] The following requirement shall apply to BS operating in Band n100 in CEPT countries. For the wanted and interfering signals coupled to the antenna connector, using the parameters in table 7.7.5.1-1 and 7.7.5.1-2, the throughput shall be ≥ 95 % of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel.
[P2740] Table 7.7.5.1-1: Additional narrowband intermodulation requirement for n100
[P2742] Table 7.7.5.1-2: Interferer frequency offset for additional narrowband intermodulation requirement for n100
[P2744] 7.8	In-channel selectivity
[P2745] 7.8.1	Definition and applicability
[P2746] In-channel selectivity (ICS) is a measure of the receiver ability to receive a wanted signal at its assigned resource block locations at the antenna connector for BS type 1-C or TAB connector for BS type 1-H in the presence of an interfering signal received at a larger power spectral density. In this condition a throughput requirement shall be met for a specified reference measurement channel. The interfering signal shall be an NR signal which is time aligned with the wanted signal.
[P2747] 7.8.2	Minimum requirement
[P2748] The minimum requirements for BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H are in TS 38.104 [2], clause 7.8.2.
[P2749] 7.8.3	Test purpose
[P2750] The purpose of this test is to verify the BS receiver ability to suppress the IQ leakage.
[P2751] 7.8.4	Method of test
[P2752] 7.8.4.1	Initial conditions
[P2753] Test environment: Normal; see annex B.2.
[P2754] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2755] 7.8.4.2	Procedure
[P2756] The minimum requirement is applied to all connectors under test.
[P2757] For BS type 1-H the procedure is repeated until all TAB connectors necessary to demonstrate conformance have been tested; see clause 7.1.
[P2758] 1)	Set the signal generator for the wanted signal to transmit as specified from table 7.8.5-1 to 7.8.5-3, as well as table 7.8.5-1a to 7.8.5-3a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2759] 2)	Set the signal generator for the interfering signal to transmit at the frequency offset and as specified from table 7.8.5-1 to 7.8.5-3, as well as table 7.8.5-1a to 7.8.5-3a for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2760] 3)	Measure the throughput according to annex A.1, as well as annex A.14 of TS 36.141 [24] for a BS declared to be capable of NB-IoT operation in NR in-band (D.41).
[P2761] In addition, for a multi-band connector, the following steps shall apply:
[P2762] 4)	For multi-band connector and single band tests, repeat the steps above per involved band where single band test configurations and test models shall apply with no carrier activated in the other band.
[P2763] 7.8.5	Test requirements
[P2764] For BS type 1-C and BS type 1-H, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channel as specified in annex A.1 with parameters specified in table 7.8.5-1 for Wide Area BS except for band n104, in table 7.8.5-1b for Wide Area BS for band n104, in table 7.8.5-2 for Medium Range BS except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104, in table 7.8.5-2b for Medium Range BS for band n46, in table 7.8.5-2c for Medium Range BS for band n96 and n102, in table 7.8.5-2d for Medium Range BS for band n104, in table 7.8.5-3 for Local Area BS except for band n46, n96, n102 and n104, in table 7.8.5-3b for Local Area BS for band n46, in table 7.8.5-3c for Local Area BS for band n96 and n102, and in table 7.8.5-3d for Local Area BS for band 104. The characteristics of the interfering signal is further specified in annex E.
[P2765] For NB-IoT operation in NR in-band, the throughput shall be ≥ 95% of the maximum throughput of the NB-IoT reference measurement channel as specified in Annex A of TS 36.141 [24] with parameters specified in table 7.8.5-1a for Wide Area BS, in table 7.8.5-2a for Medium Range BS and in table 7.8.5-3a for Local Area BS.
[P2766] Table 7.8.5-1: Wide Area BS in-channel selectivity
[P2768] Table 7.8.5-1a: Wide Area BS in-channel selectivity for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band
[P2770] Table 7.8.5-1b: Wide Area BS in-channel selectivity for band n104
[P2772] Table 7.8.5-2: Medium Range BS in-channel selectivity
[P2774] Table 7.8.5-2a: Medium Range BS in-channel selectivity for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band
[P2776] Table 7.8.5-2b: Medium Range BS in-channel selectivity for band n46
[P2778] Table 7.8.5-2c: Medium Range BS in-channel selectivity for band n96 and n102
[P2780] Table 7.8.5-2d: Medium Range BS in-channel selectivity for band n104
[P2782] Table 7.8.5-3: Local area BS in-channel selectivity
[P2784] Table 7.8.5-3a: Local Area BS in-channel selectivity for NB-IoT operation in NR in-band
[P2786] Table 7.8.5-3b: Local Area BS in-channel selectivity for band n46
[P2788] Table 7.8.5-3c: Local Area BS in-channel selectivity for band n96 and n102
[P2790] Table 7.8.5-3d: Local area BS in-channel selectivity for band n104
[P2792] 8	Conducted performance characteristics
[P2793] 8.1	General
[P2794] 8.1.1	Scope and definitions
[P2795] Conducted performance requirements specify the ability of the BS type 1-C or BS type 1-H to correctly demodulate signals in various conditions and configurations. Conducted performance requirements are specified at the antenna connector(s) (for BS type 1-C) and at the TAB connector(s) (for BS type 1-H).
[P2796] Conducted performance requirements for the BS are specified for the fixed reference channels and the propagation conditions defined in TS 38.104 [2] annex A and annex H, respectively. The requirements only apply to those FRCs that are supported by the BS.
[P2797] Unless stated otherwise, performance requirements apply for a single carrier only. Performance requirements for a BS supporting CA are defined in terms of single carrier requirements.
[P2798] For FDD operation the requirements in clause 8 shall be met with the transmitter units associated with antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) in the operating band turned ON.
[P2799] NOTE:	In normal operating conditions antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) in FDD operation are configured to transmit and receive at the same time. The associated transmitter unit(s) may be OFF for some of the tests.
[P2800] In tests performed with signal generators a synchronization signal may be provided between the BS and the signal generator, to enable correct timing of the wanted signal.
[P2801] The SNR used in this clause is specified based on a single carrier and defined as:
[P2802] SNR = S / N
[P2803] Where:
[P2804] S	is the total signal energy in a slot on a single antenna connector (for BS type 1-C) or on a single TAB connector (for BS type 1-H).
[P2805] N	is the noise energy in a bandwidth corresponding to the transmission bandwidth over the same duration where signal energy exists.
[P2806] 8.1.2	Applicability rule
[P2807] 8.1.2.0	General
[P2808] Unless otherwise stated, for a BS supporting more than 8 antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) (see D.37 in table 4.6-1), the performance requirement tests for 8 RX antennas shall apply, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2809] Unless otherwise stated, for a BS supporting different numbers of antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) (see D.37 in table 4.6-1), the tests with low MIMO correlation level shall apply only for the lowest and highest numbers of supported connectors, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2810] Unless otherwise stated, for a BS supporting different numbers of antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) (see D.37 in table 4.6-1), the 4 Tx antenna tests with low MIMO correlation level shall apply only for the highest numbers of supported connectors which is larger or equal to 4, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2811] 8.1.2.1	Applicability of PUSCH performance requirements
[P2812] 8.1.2.1.1	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2813] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2814] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests with 30% of maximum throughput shall apply only for the lowest subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1) for each frequency range.
[P2815] 8.1.2.1.2	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2816] For each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the test requirements for a specific channel bandwidth shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2817] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the tests shall be done only for the widest supported channel bandwidth. If performance requirement is not specified for this widest supported channel bandwidth, the tests shall be done by using performance requirement for the closest channel bandwidth lower than this widest supported bandwidth; the tested PRBs shall then be centered in this widest supported channel bandwidth.
[P2818] 8.1.2.1.3	Applicability of requirements for different configurations
[P2819] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests shall apply only for the mapping type declared to be supported (see D.100 in table 4.6-1). If both mapping type A and type B are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for either type A or type B; the same chosen mapping type shall then be used for all tests except the requirement for PUSCH mapping Type B with 2 symbol length allocated.
[P2820] 8.1.2.1.4	Applicability of requirements for uplink carrier aggregation
[P2821] The tests for uplink carrier aggregation shall be carried out according to the declaration (see D.107 in table 4.6-1).
[P2822] Unless otherwise stated, the tests for uplink carrier aggregation shall apply only for PUSCH with transform precoding disabled, and shall be conducted on per component carrier basis.
[P2823] 8.1.2.1.5	Applicability of requirements for TDD with different UL-DL patterns
[P2824] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, if BS supports multiple TDD UL-DL patterns, only one of the supported TDD UL-DL patterns shall be used for all tests.
[P2825] Note: For PUSCH Performance test cases, FRCs are not expected to be defined for the special slots, unless otherwise stated.
[P2826] 8.1.2.1.6	Applicability of UL timing adjustment requirements for different scenarios
[P2827] Unless otherwise stated, the tests for UL timing adjustment for scenario Y and scenario Z shall apply only if high speed train is declared to be supported (see D.109 in table 4.6-1). A BS that passes the tests for scenario Y or scenario Z, can also consider the tests for scenario X passed.
[P2828] 8.1.2.1.7	Applicability of 2-step RA type requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2829] In 2-step RA type requirements, unless otherwise stated, MsgA PUSCH tests shall be done for a BS declaring support of 2-step RA (see D.114 in table 4.6-1) only for one (freely selected) subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2830] 8.1.2.1.8	Applicability of PUSCH with 0.001% BLER requirements
[P2831] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH with 0.001% BLER requirements shall apply only for a BS declaring support of low spectral efficiency MCS index table 3 (see D.121 in table 4.6-1).
[P2832] 8.1.2.1.9	Applicability of PUSCH repetition type A requirements
[P2833] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH repetition type A requirements shall apply only for a BS declaring support of low spectral efficiency MCS index table 3 and PUSCH repetition type A (see D.121 and D.122 in table 4.6-1).
[P2834] 8.1.2.1.10	Applicability of PUSCH with enhanced DM-RS
[P2835] Unless otherwise stated, a BS declare to support PUSCH enhanced-dmrs-Type_r18 (see D.124 in table 4.6-1) and pass the requirement defined in 8.2.15, can also consider the tests defined in 8.2.1 with same configurations besides DM-RS ports as passed.
[P2836] 8.1.2.2	Applicability of PUCCH performance requirements
[P2837] 8.1.2.2.1	Applicability of requirements for different formats
[P2838] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only for each PUCCH format declared to be supported (see D.102 in table 4.6-1).
[P2839] 8.1.2.2.2	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2840] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2841] 8.1.2.2.3	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2842] For each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported by the BS, the test requirements for a specific channel bandwidth shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2843] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the tests shall be done only for the widest supported channel bandwidth. If performance requirement is not specified for this widest supported channel bandwidth, the tests shall be done by using performance requirement for the closest channel bandwidth lower than this widest supported bandwidth; the tested PRBs shall then be centered in this widest supported channel bandwidth.
[P2844] For a BS declaring to support less than 5MHz (see D.14 in table 4.6-1) a UCI BLER performance test with Format 2 PUCCH shall be tested in 3 MHz. The UCI BLER performance tests on 5MHz PUCCH Format 2 to be skipped if applicable.
[P2845] 8.1.2.2.4	Applicability of requirements for different configurations
[P2846] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH format 3 requirement tests shall apply only for the additional DM-RS configuration declared to be supported (see D.104 in table 4.6-1). If both options (without and with additional DM-RS) are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for either without or with additional DM-RS; the same chosen option shall then be used for all tests.
[P2847] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH format 4 requirement tests shall apply only for the additional DM-RS configuration declared to be supported (see D.105 in table 4.6-1). If both options (without and with additional DM-RS) are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for either without or with additional DM-RS; the same chosen option shall then be used for all tests.
[P2848] 8.1.2.2.5	Applicability of requirements for multi-slot PUCCH
[P2849] Unless otherwise stated, multi-slot PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.106 in table 4.6-1).
[P2850] 8.1.2.2.6	Applicability of requirements for PUCCH sub-slot based repetition
[P2851] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH sub-slot based repetition requirement tests shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.116 in table 4.6-1).
[P2853] 8.1.2.3	Applicability of PRACH performance requirements
[P2854] 8.1.2.3.1	Applicability of requirements for different formats
[P2855] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests shall apply only for each PRACH format declared to be supported (see D.103 in table 4.6-1).
[P2856] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests for high speed train shall apply only for each PRACH formats declared to be supported (see D.110 in table 4.6-1).
[P2857] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests for HAPS scenario shall apply only for each PRACH formats declared to be supported (see D.125 in table 4.6-1).
[P2858] 8.1.2.3.2	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2859] Unless otherwise stated, for each PRACH format with short sequence declared to be supported, for each FR, the tests shall apply only for the smallest supported subcarrier spacing in the FR (see D.103 in table 4.6-1).
[P2860] 8.1.2.3.3	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2861] Unless otherwise stated, for the subcarrier spacing to be tested, the test requirements shall apply only for anyone channel bandwidth declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2862] Unless otherwise stated, for BS supporting less than 5MHz carrier bandwidth (see D.14 in table 4.6-1), only test requirements relating to RACH preamble formats with 15kHz SCS with sequence length LRA=139, and PRACH formats with 1.25kHz SCS with sequence length LRA=839 shall apply.
[P2863] 8.1.2.3.4	Applicability of requirements for different restricted set types of long PRACH format 0
[P2864] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests for long PRACH preamble format 0 with restricted set Type A and B shall apply only for the restricted set type declared to be supported (see D.110 in table 4.6-1). If both restricted set type A and type B are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for type B; the same chosen mapping type shall then be used for all tests.
[P2865] 8.1.2.4	Applicability of PUSCH for high speed train performance requirements
[P2866] 8.1.2.4.1	Applicability of requirements for different speeds
[P2867] Unless otherwise stated, a BS that declares to support 500km/h (see D.109 in table 4.6-1) and passes the tests for 500km/h, can also consider the tests for 350km/h as passed.
[P2868] 8.1.2.4.2	Applicability of requirements for 1T1R
[P2869] In high speed train requirements, unless otherwise stated, for a BS supporting different numbers of antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) (see D.37 in table 4.6-1), if the BS supports 1RX, the tests with low MIMO correlation level shall apply only for either one connector or the second lowest number of supported connectors, in addition to the highest numbers of supported connectors, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2870] If the BS doesn't support 1RX, the tests with low MIMO correlation level shall apply only for the lowest and highest numbers of supported connectors, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2871] Note: The highest number of connectors can simultaneously be second lowest number.
[P2872] 8.1.2.5	Applicability of interlaced PUSCH performance requirements
[P2873] 8.1.2.5.1	General applicability of interlaced PUSCH performance requirements
[P2874] Interlaced PUSCH requirement tests shall apply only for a BS declaring support of interlaced formats (see D.111 in table 4.6-1).
[P2875] 8.1.2.5.2	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2876] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2877] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier-spacing declared to be supported for interlaced PUSCH, the tests shall apply only for the supported subcarrier spacing. If both 15kHz and 30kHz SCS are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for 30kHz SCS (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2878] 8.1.2.5.3	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2879] For each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the tests for a specific channel bandwidth shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2880] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the tests shall be done only for the widest supported channel bandwidth. If performance requirement is not specified for this widest supported channel bandwidth, the tests shall be done by using performance requirement defined for 20 MHz channel bandwidth. For 15kHz subcarrier spacing, the tested RB’s are uniformly spaced over the channel bandwidth at RB index {110, 120, …, 210}. For 30kHz subcarrier spacing, the tested RB’s are uniformly spaced over the channel bandwidth at RB index {55, 60, …, 105}.
[P2881] 8.1.2.5.4	Applicability of requirements for different configurations
[P2882] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests shall apply only for the mapping type declared to be supported (see D.100 in table 4.6-1). If both mapping type A and type B are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for either type A or type B; the same chosen mapping type shall then be used for all tests.
[P2883] 8.1.2.5.5	Applicability of CG-UCI multiplexed on PUSCH requirements
[P2884] Unless otherwise stated, interlaced CG-UCI multiplexed on interlaced PUSCH requirements shall apply only for a BS declaring support of CG-UCI (see D.113 in table 4.6-1).
[P2885] 8.1.2.6	Applicability of interlaced PUCCH performance requirements
[P2886] 8.1.2.6.1	General applicability of interlaced PUCCH performance requirements
[P2887] Interlaced PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only for a BS declaring support of interlaced formats (see D.111 in table 4.6-1).
[P2888] 8.1.2.6.2	Applicability of requirements for different formats
[P2889] Unless otherwise stated, interlaced PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only for each interlaced PUCCH format declared to be supported (see D.102 in table 4.6-1).
[P2890] 8.1.2.6.3	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2891] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2892] 8.1.2.6.4	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2893] For each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported by the BS, the tests for a specific channel bandwidth shall apply only if the BS supports it (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2894] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, the tests shall be done only for the widest supported channel bandwidth. If performance requirement is not specified for this widest supported channel bandwidth, the tests shall be done by using performance requirement defined for 20 MHz channel bandwidth. For 15kHz subcarrier spacing, the tested RB’s are uniformly spaced over the channel bandwidth at RB index {110, 120, …, 210} for PUCCH formats 0, 1, and 2, and {110, 120, …, 200} for PUCCH format 3. For 30kHz subcarrier spacing, the tested RB’s are uniformly spaced over the channel bandwidth at RB index {55, 60, …, 105} for PUCCH formats 0, 1, and 2, and {55, 60, …, 100} for PUCCH format 3.
[P2895] 8.1.2.7	Applicability of performance requirements for PRACH with LRA =1151 and LRA =571
[P2896] 8.1.2.7.1	Applicability of requirements for different formats
[P2897] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests shall apply only for each PRACH format declared to be supported (see D.112 in table 4.6-1).
[P2898] 8.1.2.7.2	Applicability of requirements for different subcarrier spacings
[P2899] Unless otherwise stated, for each PRACH format with LRA =1151 and LRA =571 declared to be supported, the tests shall apply only for the supported subcarrier spacing. If both 15kHz and 30kHz SCS are declared to be supported, the tests shall be done for 30kHz SCS (see D.112 in table 4.6-1).
[P2900] 8.1.2.7.3	Applicability of requirements for different channel bandwidths
[P2901] Unless otherwise stated, for the subcarrier spacing to be tested, the tests shall apply only for anyone channel bandwidth not less than 20MHz declared to be supported (see D.14 in table 4.6-1).
[P2902] 8.1.2.8	Applicability of performance requirements for PUSCH with TB over Multi-Slots
[P2903] 8.1.2.8.1	Applicability of requirements for different UL-DL patterns
[P2904] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH with TB over Multi-slots requirement tests shall apply only for UL-DL pattern characters declared to be supported (see D.117 and D.118 in table 4.6-1).
[P2905] Unless otherwise stated, for each UL-DL patten character declared to be supported, if BS supports multiple TDD UL-DL patterns, only one of the supported TDD UL-DL patterns shall be used for PUSCH with TB over Multi-slots performance tests.
[P2906] 8.1.2.9	Applicability of performance requirements for PUSCH with DM-RS bundling
[P2907] 8.1.2.9.1	Applicability of requirements for TDD with different subcarrier spacings
[P2908] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH with DM-RS bundling requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spcaing declared to be supported (see D.119 and D.120 in table 4.6-1).
[P2909] 8.1.2.9.2	Applicability of requirements for TDD with different UL-DL patterns
[P2910] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, if BS supports multiple TDD UL-DL patterns, only one of the supported TDD UL-DL patterns with more than one concecutive UL slots shall be used for the PUSCH with DM-RS bundling performance tests.
[P2911] 8.1.2.9.3	Applicability of requirements for different receiver antenna connectors
[P2912] Unless otherwise stated, for a BS supporting different numbers of antenna connectors (for BS type 1-C) or TAB connectors (for BS type 1-H) (see D.37 in table 4.6-1), the PUSCH with DM-RS bundling performance tests with low MIMO correlation level shall apply only for the highest numbers of supported connectors, and the specific connectors used for testing are based on manufacturer declaration.
[P2913] 8.1.2.10	Applicability of performance requirements for PUCCH with DM-RS bundling
[P2914] 8.1.2.10.1	Applicability of requirements for TDD with different subcarrier spacings
[P2915] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH with DM-RS bundling requirement tests shall apply only for each subcarrier spcaing declared to be supported (see D.119 and D.120 in table 4.6-1).
[P2916] 8.1.2.10.2	Applicability of requirements for TDD with different UL-DL patterns
[P2917] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, if BS supports multiple TDD UL-DL patterns, only one of the supported TDD UL-DL patterns with more than one concecutive UL slots shall be used for the PUCCH with DM-RS bundling performance tests.
[P2918] 8.1.2.11	Applicability of PUSCH performance requirements for ATG scenario
[P2919] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests in clause 8.2.1, 8.2.2 and 8.2.3 with 1Tx test cases shall apply only for the BS declared to be supported (see D.123 in table 4.6-1).
[P2920] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests in clause 8.2.14 shall apply only for the BS declared to be supported (see D.123 in table 4.6-1).
[P2921] 8.1.2.11.1	Applicability of requirements with different UL-DL patterns
[P2922] Unless otherwise stated, for each subcarrier spacing declared to be supported, if BS supports multiple TDD UL-DL patterns including new TDD pattern (e.g., 30D4S6U, S= 14G) for ATG scenario, only one of the supported TDD UL-DL patterns shall be used for all tests.
[P2923] Note:	For PUSCH Performance test cases, FRCs are not expected to be defined for the special slots, unless otherwise stated.
[P2924] 8.1.2.11.2	Applicability of requirements for different MCSs
[P2925] Unless otherwise stated, PUSCH requirement tests with 256QAM in clause 8.2.14 shall apply only for the BS declared to be supported (see D.123 in table 4.6-1).  A BS that declares to support 256QAM, and passes the test with 256QAM and the test with 64QAM in clause 8.2.1, the PUSCH requirement tests with 64QAM in clause 8.2.14 can be skipped.
[P2926] 8.1.2.12	Applicability of PUCCH performance requirements for ATG scenario
[P2927] Unless otherwise stated, PUCCH requirement tests in clauses 8.3.1 to 8.3.6 shall apply only for the BS declared to be supported (see D.123 in table 4.6-1).
[P2928] 8.1.2.13	Applicability of PRACH performance requirements for ATG scenario
[P2929] Unless otherwise stated, PRACH requirement tests in clauses 8.4.1.5 shall apply only for the BS declared to be supported (see D.123 in table 4.6-1).
[P2930] 8.2	Performance requirements for PUSCH
[P2931] 8.2.1	Performance requirements for PUSCH with transform precoding disabled
[P2932] 8.2.1.1	Definition and applicability
[P2933] The performance requirement of PUSCH is determined by a minimum required throughput for a given SNR. The required throughput is expressed as a fraction of maximum throughput for the FRCs listed in annex A. The performance requirements assume HARQ re-transmissions.
[P2934] Which specific test(s) are applicable to BS is based on the test applicability rules defined in clause 8.1.2.1.
[P2935] 8.2.1.2	Minimum Requirement
[P2936] The minimum requirement is in TS 38.104 [2] clause 8.2.1.
[P2937] 8.2.1.3	Test Purpose
[P2938] The test shall verify the receiver's ability to achieve throughput under multipath fading propagation conditions for a given SNR.
[P2939] 8.2.1.4	Method of test
[P2940] 8.2.1.4.1	Initial Conditions
[P2941] Test environment:	Normal, see annex B.2.
[P2942] RF channels to be tested for single carrier:	M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2943] RF channels to be tested for carrier aggregation: MBW Channel CA; see clause 4.9.1.
[P2944] 8.2.1.4.2	Procedure
[P2945] 1)	Connect the BS tester generating the wanted signal, multipath fading simulators and AWGN generators to all BS antenna connectors for diversity reception via a combining network as shown in annex D.5 and D.6 for BS type 1-C and type 1-H respectively.
[P2946] 2)	Adjust the AWGN generator, according to the channel bandwidth, defined in table 8.2.1.4.2-1.
[P2947] Table 8.2.1.4.2-1: AWGN power level at the BS input
[P2949] 3)	The characteristics of the wanted signal shall be configured according to the corresponding UL reference measurement channel defined in annex A and the test parameters in table 8.2.1.4.2-2.
[P2950] Table 8.2.1.4.2-2: Test parameters for testing PUSCH
[P2952] 4)	The multipath fading emulators shall be configured according to the corresponding channel model defined in annex G.
[P2953] 5)	Adjust the equipment so that required SNR specified in table 8.2.1.5-1 to 8.2.1.5-20 is achieved at the BS input.
[P2954] 6)	For each of the reference channels in table 8.2.1.5-1 to 8.2.1.5-20 applicable for the base station, measure the throughput.
[P2955] 8.2.1.5	Test Requirement
[P2956] The throughput measured according to clause 8.2.1.4.2 shall not be below the limits for the SNR levels specified in table 8.2.1.5-1 to 8.2.1.5-18.
[P2957] Table 8.2.1.5-1: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 5 MHz channel bandwidth, 15 kHz SCS
[P2959] Table 8.2.1.5-2: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 15 kHz SCS
[P2961] Table 8.2.1.5-3: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 20 MHz channel bandwidth, 15 kHz SCS
[P2963] Table 8.2.1.5-4: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P2965] Table 8.2.1.5-5: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 20 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P2967] Table 8.2.1.5-6: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 40 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P2969] Table 8.2.1.5-7: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 100 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS

[P2998] 8.2.2	Performance requirements for PUSCH with transform precoding enabled
[P2999] 8.2.2.1	Definition and applicability
[P3000] The performance requirement of PUSCH is determined by a minimum required throughput for a given SNR. The required throughput is expressed as a fraction of maximum throughput for the FRCs listed in annex A. The performance requirements assume HARQ re-transmissions.
[P3001] Which specific test(s) are applicable to BS is based on the test applicability rules defined in clause 8.1.2.
[P3002] 8.2.2.2	Minimum Requirement
[P3003] The minimum requirement is in TS 38.104 [2] clause 8.2.2.
[P3004] 8.2.2.3	Test Purpose
[P3005] The test shall verify the receiver's ability to achieve throughput under multipath fading propagation conditions for a given SNR.
[P3006] 8.2.2.4	Method of test
[P3007] 8.2.2.4.1	Initial Conditions
[P3008] Test environment: Normal, see annex B.2.
[P3009] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P3010] 8.2.2.4.2	Procedure
[P3011] 1)	Connect the BS tester generating the wanted signal, multipath fading simulators and AWGN generators to all BS antenna connectors for diversity reception via a combining network as shown in annex D.5 and D.6 for BS type 1-C and type 1-H respectively.
[P3012] 2)	Adjust the AWGN generator, according to the SCS and channel bandwidth, defined in table 8.2.2.4.2-1.
[P3013] Table 8.2.2.4.2-1: AWGN power level at the BS input
[P3015] 3)	The characteristics of the wanted signal shall be configured according to the corresponding UL reference measurement channel defined in annex A and the test parameters in table 8.2.2.4.2-2.
[P3016] Table 8.2.2.4.2-2: Test parameters for testing PUSCH
[P3018] 4)	The multipath fading emulators shall be configured according to the corresponding channel model defined in annex G.
[P3019] 5)	Adjust the equipment so that required SNR specified in table 8.2.2.5-1 to 8.2.2.5-4 is achieved at the BS input.
[P3020] 6)	For each of the reference channels in table 8.2.2.5-1 to 8.2.2.5-4 applicable for the base station, measure the throughput.
[P3021] 8.2.2.5	Test Requirement
[P3022] The throughput measured according to clause 8.2.2.4.2 shall not be below the limits for the SNR levels specified in table 8.2.2.5-1 to 8.2.2.5-4.
[P3023] Table 8.2.2.5-1: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 5 MHz channel bandwidth, 15 kHz SCS
[P3025] Table 8.2.2.5-2: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type A, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3027] Table 8.2.2.5-3: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type B, 5 MHz channel bandwidth, 15 kHz SCS
[P3029] Table 8.2.2.5-4: Test requirements for PUSCH with 70% of maximum throughput, Type B, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3031] 8.2.3	Performance requirements for UCI multiplexed on PUSCH
[P3032] 8.2.3.1	Definition and applicability
[P3033] The performance requirement of UCI multiplexed on PUSCH is determined by two parameters: block error probability (BLER) of CSI part 1 and block error probability of CSI part 2. The performance is measured by the required SNR at block error probability of CSI part 1 not exceeding 0.1 %, and the required SNR at block error probability of CSI part 2 not exceeding 1 %.
[P3034] The CSI part 1 BLER is defined as the probability of incorrectly decoding the CSI part 1 information when the CSI part 1 information is sent.
[P3035] The CSI part 2 BLER is defined as the probability of incorrectly decoding the CSI part 2 information when the CSI part 2 information is sent.
[P3036] In the test of UCI multiplexed on PUSCH, the UCI information only contains CSI part 1 and CSI part 2 information, there is no HACK/ACK information transmitted.
[P3037] The number of UCI information bit payload per slot is defined for two cases as follows:
[P3038] -	7 bits:  5 bits in CSI part 1, 2 bits in CSI part 2,
[P3039] -	40 bits: 20 bits in CSI part 1, 20 bits in CSI part 2.
[P3040] The 7 bits UCI information case is further defined with the bitmap [c0 c1 c2 c3 c4] = [0 1 0 1 0] for CSI part 1 information, where c0 is mapping to the RI information, and with the bitmap [c0 c1] = [1 0] for CSI part 2 information.
[P3041] The 40 bits UCI information case is assumed random information bit selection.
[P3042] In both tests, PUSCH data, CSI part 1 and CSI part 2 are transmitted simultaneously.
[P3043] Which specific test(s) is applicable to BS is based on the test applicability rule defined in clause 8.1.2.
[P3044] 8.2.3.2	Minimum Requirements
[P3045] The minimum requirements are in TS 38.104 [2] clause 8.2.3.
[P3046] 8.2.3.3	Test purpose
[P3047] The test shall verify the receiver's ability to detect UCI with CSI part 1 and CSI part 2 bits multiplexed on PUSCH under multipath fading propagation conditions for a given SNR.
[P3048] 8.2.3.4	Method of test
[P3049] 8.2.3.4.1	Initial conditions
[P3050] Test environment: Normal, see annex B.2.
[P3051] RF channels to be tested for single carrier: M; see clause 4.9.1.
[P3052] 8.2.3.4.2	Procedure
[P3053] 1)	Connect the BS tester generating the wanted signal, multipath fading simulators and AWGN generators to all BS antenna connectors for diversity reception via a combining network as shown in annex D.5 and D.6 for BS type 1-C and type 1-H respectively.
[P3054] 2)	Adjust the AWGN generator, according to combination of SCS and channel bandwidth defined in table 8.2.3.4.2-1.
[P3055] Table 8.2.3.4.2-1: AWGN power level at the BS input
[P3057] 3)	The characteristics of the wanted signal shall be configured according to the corresponding UL reference measurement channel defined in annex A and the specific test parameters are configured as below. The UCI information bit payload per slot is equal to 7 bits with CSI part 1 5bits, CSI part 2 2bit; and the UCI information bit payload per slot is equal to 40 bits with CSI part 1 20bits, CSI part 2 20bits.
[P3058] Table: 8.2.3.4.2-2: Test parameters for testing UCI multiplexed on PUSCH
[P3060] 4)	The multipath fading emulators shall be configured according to the corresponding channel model defined in annex G.
[P3061] 5)	Adjust the equipment so that required SNR specified in table 8.2.3.5-1 to 8.2.3.5-4 is achieved at the BS input during the UCI multiplexed on PUSCH transmissions.
[P3062] 6)	The signal generators sends a test pattern where UCI with CSI part 1 and CSI part 2 information can be multiplexed on PUSCH. The following statistics are kept: the number of incorrectly decoded CSI part 1 information transmission, the number of incorrectly decoded CSI part 2 information transmission during UCI multiplexed on PUSCH transmission.
[P3063] 8.2.3.5	Test Requirement
[P3064] The fractional of incorrectly decoded UCI with CSI part 1 according to clause 8.2.3.4.2 shall be less than 0.1 % for SNR listed in table 8.2.3.5-1 and table 8.2.3.5-2. The fractional of incorrectly decoded UCI with CSI part 2 according to clause 8.2.3.4.2 shall be less than 1 % for SNR listed in table 8.2.3.5-3 and table 8.2.3.5-4.
[P3065] Table 8.2.3.5-1: Test requirements for UCI multiplexed on PUSCH, Type A, CSI part 1, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3067] Table 8.2.3.5-2: Test requirements for UCI multiplexed on PUSCH, Type B, CSI part 1, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3069] Table 8.2.3.5-3: Test requirements for UCI multiplexed on PUSCH, Type A, CSI part 2, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3071] Table 8.2.3.5-4: Test requirements for UCI multiplexed on PUSCH, Type B, CSI part 2, 10 MHz channel bandwidth, 30 kHz SCS
[P3073] 8.2.4	Performance requirements for PUSCH for high speed train
[P3074] 8.2.4.1	Definition and applicability
[P3075] The performance requirement of PUSCH is determined by a minimum required throughput for a given SNR. The required throughput is expressed as a fraction of maximum throughput for the FRCs listed in annex A. The performance requirements assume HARQ re-transmissions. The performance requirements for high speed train conditions are optional.
[P3076] Which specific test(s) are applicable to BS is based on the test applicability rules defined in clause 8.1.2.1 and clause 8.1.2.4.
[P3077] The performance requirements for PUSCH for high speed train only apply to Wide Area Base Stations and Medium Range Base Stations (Subject to declaration).

[P3092] 3)	The characteristics of the wanted signal shall be configured according to the corresponding UL reference measurement channel defined in annex A and the test parameters in table 8.2.4.4.2-2.
[P3093] Table 8.2.4.4.2-2: Test parameters for testing high speed train PUSCH
[P3095] 4)	The channel simulators shall be configured according to the corresponding channel model defined in annex G.
[P3096] 5)	Adjust the equipment so that required SNR specified in table 8.2.4.5-1 to 8.2.4.5-10 is achieved at the BS input.
[P3097] 6)	For each of the reference channels in table 8.2.4.5-1 to 8.2.4.5-10 applicable for the base station, measure the throughput.
[P3098] 8.2.4.5	Test Requirement
[P3099] The throughput measured according to clause 8.2.4.4.2 shall not be below the limits for the SNR levels specified in table 8.2.4.5-1 to 8.2.4.5-10. Unless stated otherwise, the MIMO correlation matrices for the gNB are defined in annex G for low correlation.
